# charkit code map

Generated by `python -m charkit.codemap` from the code (docstrings, public names, the QA registry, the command line);
regenerate it after changing a module's docstring or public functions. The curated part is this header; everything below
"## Modules" is generated. Usage lives in each module's docstring: read it before the code.

## Where things live

- **The build** (`python -m charkit build`, charkit/cli.py): the spec resolved (manifest.py, refs.py), the venv steps
  (cli.code_head, code_body, geom_hair, hair_select, pieces_hair, garments_geom: each a cache.file_step), then Blender
  (build_blender.py drives scene.py's stages: character.py assembles the head and body, hair, garments, shading), the
  geometry bundle (bundle.py), the QA (qa3d.run), the boards, the look export (gltf.py). The cache: cache.py.
- **Builders** (Blender side and their venv twins): character.py, head.py, code_base.py (the authored head),
  code_body.py / body.py (the body), hair.py, geom/hairpieces.py (the hair pieces), garments.py and outfit.py (the
  outfit), accessories.py, eyes.py, mouth.py, expressions.py, shade.py / faceshade.py (materials, face shading),
  geomstage.py (the garments' venv stage), base_anime.py (the anime base mesh).
- **Fast evaluators and fits**: bodyeval.py (numpy body, garments, hair: the sweep's garments stage), faceeval.py,
  fitkit.py, bodyfit.py, facefit.py, tune.py, triage.py, hairlab.py (the hair pieces rebuilt and measured).
- **QA parts**: registered with `@qa_part` (registry.py; table below), run by qa3d.run on a bundle. Shape IoU per
  piece and view: qa3d.sheet_pieces (piece_*), qa3d.hair_pieces (hair_piece_*). Flag checks carry `flag`
  (registry.flag_check). Declared checks (a family, a piece, limits): declared.py. Measurement steps: steps/*.py.
- **Calibration**: calibrate.py (the triple: design moved 1-2 px, known-bad, random floor; records in
  calib/records), the stand-ins in calib/*.py (labels.Garments, details.Details, ...), declared.Declared (generic).
- **Variants and attribution**: sweep.py (`charkit sweep`: declared rows rebuilt at a stage, measured, tabulated with
  the shape guard; swap mode); optimize.py (`charkit sweep optimize`: a fit's knobs tuned by CMA-ES over sweep rows on
  persistent workers, the constraints enforced, the best confirmed by real builds).
- **Gate and infrastructure**: gate.py (policy K), pregate.py, codediff.py, closure.py, history.py, remote.py and
  boxjob.py (the boxes), bucketsync.py, procs.py (build slots), worker.py, sparse.py, trace.py.
- **Review pages**: reviewpage.py (`charkit review page`: the standard page), preview.py (per-merge previews),
  review.py, flags.py, checkpoint.py, *page.py.
- **Simulation**: sim/ (xpbd.py cloth, springbone.py, drape.py, motion.py, motionqa.py).
- **Renderer**: render/ (charkit's WebGPU toon renderer: gpu.py, buffers.py, views.py, softras.py, parity.py);
  qarender.py (the QA's drawing through it).
- **Geometry kernel**: geom/ (mesh IO, repair, booleans, BVH, raster, loft, hull, solidify, subsurf).

## Modules

### charkit/

#### `charkit/__init__.py`

charkit: a parametric anime character kit (docs/CHARKIT.md). Import inside Blender with the repo root on sys.path.

#### `charkit/accessories.py`

Hair accessories and small props (docs/CHARKIT.md §2): meshes placed on the hair and oriented to it. Kinds: 'bun' (stacked rounded pads, the hair's own material), 'star' (a sparkle clip: n major points, optional minor points between them, curved edges, a raised faceted middle), 'crab' (a little crab clip: a flattened body, two raised notched claws, eyes ...

- `rounded_box(sx, sy, sz, rad, segs=4)`: a rounded box centred at the origin (a subdivided cube pushed onto the rounded shape). -> (verts, quads).
- `sphere(c, r, nu=12, nv=8)`: a UV sphere: centre c, radii r (scalar or 3) -> (verts, faces: quads and pole triangles).
- `tube(P, rad, sides=8)`: a capped tube along a polyline P (k, 3) with a radius per point -> (verts, faces).
- `star_outline(shape=None)`: a star's outline in its plane (x right, y up; the height tip to tip = up + down) -> (k, 2) points, tips and the ...
- `star(shape=None)`: a star clip, its back on the z = 0 plane, facing +z: the outline (star_outline) as a rim `thick` deep, the middle ...
- `capsule(P, rad, sides=8, nv=6)`: a tube along a polyline P with a radius per point, its ends rounded (a sphere at each end) -> (verts, faces).
- `pincer(c, r, up, notch, cut, depth, long=1.0, rings=(1.0, 0.92, 0.75, 0.5, ...)`: a crab's claw: a flat-backed dome (its back on z = c's, rising `depth` in front) over an outline round c, radius ...

#### `charkit/boarddiff.py`

Two builds' pictures and QA compared exactly: each PNG under boards/ and qa/ and the build's sheets (the max and mean absolute difference per image, 0-255 per channel) and each QA check's value and status (qa/qa.json). Prints every image and every changed check, and exits 1 on any difference: a change to how the boards render (such as qa.render_views) ...

- `pngs(d)`
- `image_diff(a, b)`: -> (max, mean) absolute difference of two PNGs' RGBA pixels; (None, None) if their sizes differ.
- `qa_diff(a, b)`: -> [(check, (value, status) in a, in b)] for each check whose value or status differs.
- `main(argv)`

The body: MakeHuman's CC0 base mesh -> macro morph -> anime proportions -> the realistic head removed at the neck -> scaled to the spec's height and head count -> a VRM 1.0 humanoid armature (fingers included) with merged skin weights.

#### `charkit/boxjob.py`

Box jobs that outlive their ssh, and the boxes' load, measured (standard library only, Python 3.9+: the box runs this file with its system python3).

- `jdir(jid)`
- `alive(pid)`
- `pids(d)`: (supervisor pid, job pid) from the job's pid file, or (None, None).
- `exit_of(d)`: (rc, ended epoch) once the job has ended, else None.
- `state(d)`: 'pending' (not started), 'running', 'done' or 'lost' (no exit code and no supervisor: the box stopped under it).
- `descendants(pid)`
- `start(jid)`: launch the job detached, once. A retried start (the first one's ssh dropped) finds the claim and only reports.
- `supervise(jid)`: (detached) run the job, record its exit code, publish the load log.
- `kill(jid)`
- `follow(jid, offset=0, out=None, heartbeat=HEARTBEAT, poll=0.5)`: the log from byte offset as frames until the job ends (see the module's docstring).
- class `Frames`: the laptop's side of follow: feed it bytes as they arrive; it hands log bytes to on_data as soon as they come (a ...
- `info(d)`
- `list_jobs(days=1)`
- `prune(days=KEEP_DAYS)`: finished or lost jobs' directories older than days.
- `cron_lines(dst)`: the sampler's user crontab lines: once a minute, and at boot (a `boot` event).
- `cron_merge(cur, dst)`: the crontab text `cur` with the sampler's lines (cron_lines) added where missing; another line of ours (another ...
- `install_sampler(d)`: this file into JOBS/bin (when newer than what's there) and the user crontab lines running its `sample` each ...
- `slots_now(slots_dir=SLOTS_DIR)`: the build slots: count, held (with who holds them) and the builds waiting for one.
- `reading(slots_dir=SLOTS_DIR)`: what charkit.remote.pick_box routes by, now: the build slots (slots_now) with the box's cores (ncpu), its 1-, 5- ...
- `running_jobs()`
- `sample(load_dir=LOAD, slots_dir=SLOTS_DIR, now=None, boot=False)`: one sample appended to load_dir/load-<UTC day>.jsonl -> the sample. boot: the @reboot line's (event 'boot').
- `publish_load(d, meta)`: the load log into the bucket under load-<host> (the job's own bucketsync.py), when a job ends.
- `main(argv)`

#### `charkit/boxload.py`

Each box's load, summarised: the numbers for the build-box capacity call (more slots, a bigger machine, or a second box) that the integrator brings to Michael. The samples are charkit/boxjob.py's, taken once a minute on the box and published to the bucket (load-<VM>) whenever a job ends; slot waits are charkit.procs's (slots/waits.jsonl, one line per ...

- `pct(xs, q)`
- `mean(xs)`
- `read(d, since)`: samples and waits from a pulled load directory, from epoch `since` on.
- `summarise(samples, waits)`: the numbers (see the module's docstring) -> dict.
- `gaps(samples)`: the gaps of GAP s or more between consecutive samples, each read by the kernel's boot time the samples carry ...
- `reading(s)`: what binds, from the numbers (a hint for the capacity call, not the call).
- `hourly(samples)`: per UTC hour: CPU busy mean, load peak, slots held mean, the longest queue.
- `text(name, s, hours_rows=None)`
- `main(args, BOX)`: `remote load`: pull each box's samples from the bucket (or have it publish now: --fresh) and summarise.

#### `charkit/brows.py`

Anime brows (docs/CHARKIT.md §2, face features): a tapered ribbon over each eye on the face surface, knob-driven (height, arch, length, thickness, tilt), with expression keys (raise, angry, sad, surprised, worried, relaxed, focus, knit, pained) from the same curve. Drawn over the hair later (the hair phase gives them their own layer); here they sit just ...

- `curve(BK, EK, L, t)`: brow-local (x, z) of the brow's centre line at t (0 inner .. 1 outer).
- `thickness(BK, L, t)`
- `ribbon(F, BK, EK, L, side, eye_c, n=32, knobs=None)`: the brow as a ribbon (verts, quads) centred on its curve.
- `expressions(BK)`: named brow shapes as knob overrides.

#### `charkit/bucketsync.py`

Bulk data between the laptop and the boxes through their bucket, not the IAP tunnel (1-3 MB/s, and ssh drops under load). A content-addressed store in the boxes' existing bucket: every file is a blob named by its sha256 (<bucket>/cas/<aa>/<sha>), a manifest (itself a blob, cas/m/<sha>) lists a tree's paths, blobs, modes and mtimes, and each side moves ...

- class `Token`: an access token: the box's service account (metadata server) or the laptop user's (gcloud), kept until it expires ...
- `gcloud_config()`: the laptop's gcloud config for the box: CLOUDSDK_CONFIG (build.sh exports its env file's), else the env file's ...
- class `Bucket`: the GCS JSON API over kept-alive HTTPS connections (a pool the threads share).
- `blob(sha)`
- `mblob(sha)`
- `file_sha(path)`
- class `Known`: the blobs this side knows the bucket has (it uploaded or checked them): a text file of shas, appended.

- `lacking(bk, need, known)`: the blobs in need the bucket lacks (those this side doesn't already know it has are checked).
- `upload_blobs(bk, need, known, path_of, miss=None)`: the blobs in need the bucket lacks, uploaded in parallel (biggest first) -> (count, bytes).

#### `charkit/checkpoint.py`

The checkpoint review page (docs/CHARKIT_HANDOFF.md, step 5): one local HTML page, opened in the browser, with the design (the generated references, the manifest's sheets) next to our builds view by view, the QA across the builds, and the open decisions. Nothing to find or arrange by hand: every picture is cut, scaled alike and labelled.

#### `charkit/codediff.py`

Code compared between two trees at the level of definitions, with the build cache's code walk (charkit.cache.code_units: functions, classes and each module's top-level statements, followed as Python scopes names). Two uses in the merge gate (charkit/gate.py):

- `tree(t, repo=ROOT)`: a cache.Tree for t: a Tree, a directory (a worktree), or a git revision or tree id in repo.
- `module_of(rel)`: 'charkit/geom/hull.py' -> 'charkit.geom.hull' ('__init__.py': the package).
- `part_defs(t)`: the QA parts a tree registers, read with ast (nothing imported): [{name, module, fn, prefix, keep, skip_key}] ...
- `measure_units(t)`: each QA part's measuring code in tree t -> {part: {unit: digest}}: the part's function and what it reaches, and ...
- `measure_changes(a, b)`: the QA parts whose measuring code differs between trees a and b (a part new in b is new, not changed) -> {part: ...
- `part_checks(qa, part)`: the checks one part reported in a build's qa.json (qa: the report): its record (measured.part_checks, written ...
- `defs(t, paths=None)`: every definition in tree t (or in the charkit .py files `paths` only) -> {unit: digest}, as code_units' fine walk ...
- `changed(a, b, paths=None, repo=ROOT)`: the definitions that differ between trees a and b (added, removed or changed) -> sorted units.
- `reach(t, units)`: what the definitions reach in tree t (themselves included; code_units' walk from each) -> set of units ...
- `interacts(move, change, move_trees, change_trees, symmetric=True)`: whether two code changes meet (`gate --carry`'s rule): the move's changed definitions among what the change's ...
- `git_changes(repo, a, b)`: [(status, path)] between two revisions or trees (closure.changes' shape).

- `modules(root=ROOT)`: every charkit module (package, relative path) under the PACKAGES, sorted.
- `read(root, rel)`: a module's map: its docstring's opening, public functions and classes (first docstring lines), QA parts, ...
- `commands(root=ROOT)`: the command line's commands: `python -m charkit CMD` -> the module it calls (cli.main's dispatch, read with ast) ...
- `generate(root=ROOT)`: the map's text for a tree.
- `main(args)`

#### `charkit/fit_blender.py`

The Blender side of `python -m charkit fit` (charkit/facefit.py): what the numpy face evaluator (charkit/faceeval.py) can't make itself, saved once as arrays.

#### `charkit/fitkit.py`

Fitting spec knobs to QA checks, generically: the machinery charkit/facefit.py uses for the face, eyes and neck, and a body, garment or hair fitter can use for its own (docs/CHARKIT.md §4).

- class `Knob`
- class `Term`
- `with_knobs(spec, x, knobs)`
- `residuals(checks, terms, authority=None)`: -> [dict(name, view, measure, ref, r (in tolerances), w (weight), value)].
- `bands(res)`: each residual's status band at the start: 1 (it passes: keep it inside 1), its WARN line (it warns), or None.
- `vector(res, x=None, knobs=(), keep=None)`: residuals (and the knob values, for the regulariser; keep: bands(start) to protect) -> the least-squares vector.
- `vector_jacobian(res, dres, knobs=(), keep=None)`: vector()'s Jacobian from each residual's gradient dres (terms x knobs, knob units): the weighted residual, its ...
- `cost(res, x=None, knobs=(), loss='linear', keep=None)`: 0.5 sum of the loss over the vector, as scipy's least_squares counts it ('linear' or 'soft_l1' at LOSS_SCALE).
- `loss_of(f, loss='linear')`: cost's loss over a least-squares vector.
- `plain(x)`: a check set made json- and pickle-safe (arrays and private keys dropped, numpy scalars as floats).
- class `Pool`: evaluations in worker processes, each holding its own evaluator (factory 'module:Class', built with args).
- `measures(checks)`: the numbers a check set holds, flat: 'check', 'check.sub' (ratios, regions, per-height ratios), 'check.ours'.
- `sensitivity(pool, spec, knobs, fine=False)`: every knob one step down and up from the spec, every measured value's change.
- class `Budget`
- `optimise(pool, spec, knobs, terms, group, authority=None, budget=None, ...)`: least squares over one group's knobs and terms from the spec's values (see the module).
- `regressions(before, after, names=None)`: graded checks whose status got worse (or that disappeared), optionally only those `names` accepts.
- `guard(pool, start, fitted, knobs, before, names, steps=(0.75, 0.5, 0.25, ...)`: checks the fit doesn't aim at but mustn't break (names(check) -> bool): while any reads a worse status than at ...
- `triage(res, table, knobs, spec, thresh=0.25)`: each term still outside its tolerance after a fit, sorted into why: 'needs a knob' (no knob moves it by `thresh` ...

The tune loop's fitters (docs/CHARKIT.md §4): each fast fitter behind one interface, declaring which checks it targets and which knobs it owns, so the loop can run them in turn and the triage can say who moves what.

- `get(spec, path, default=None)`: a value by path (a tuple of keys); list items by index, name or kind.
- `put(spec, path, value, source=None)`: set a value by path in place, creating dict levels; a list item is found by index, name or kind.
- `dotted(path)`
- `inventory(spec, prefix=())`: every numeric knob in a spec -> {dotted path: value}.
- `merge_args(base, extra)`: build options: `extra` replaces `base`'s value of the same flag (['--hair', 'mesh'] + ['--hair', 'geom']).

- `probe_headroom(table, spec, terms, knobs, authority=None)`: from a sensitivity table (charkit.sensitivity/1), the fit objective at the table's point and at each knob's step ...
- `interpolate(spec_path, fitted_path, knobs, t, out_path)`: the spec with every fitted knob moved a fraction t of the way from its start to the fit (a shorter step along the ...
- class `BodyFitter`: charkit.bodyfit (tool/bodyfit): the body, garments, hair and palette fitted to the silhouette, model-sheet body, ...
- class `OptionsFitter`: build options as a discrete choice: each option in the character's tune config (`options`: {name, set: {spec ...
- `registry(config=None, budget=None, only=None, workers=None)`: the fitters in the order the loop runs them (budget: each fast fitter's evaluations).

#### `charkit/gate.py`

The merge gate: what a branch would do to the integration branch, measured before it lands. Nothing is merged and no branch moves: throwaway worktrees at the integration head take the baseline and, with `git merge --no-commit`, the candidate; the tests run, each side is built when it has to be, and the two builds' QA and traces are compared.

- class `Clock`: the gate's phases: each one's start (s since the gate began), length and a note; phases may overlap.
- `build_steps(out, wall=None)`: a build's time by part, from its trace: blender (the Blender stages and the bundle), qa (the venv's QA), and ...
- `compare_qa(a, b, remeasured=None)`: per check: baseline -> candidate, with a verdict (regressed, improved, value, new, gone, removed, ungraded; ...
- `produce_inputs(tree, spec)`: the manifest's produced references (produced_by: the hull, the outfit masks, the hair layers) made in tree by its ...
- `rebased_bundle(bundle_dir, tmp)`: a bundle whose spec's paths into its build's out folder (the hair pieces' report, the code head and body, the ...
- `cross_qa(tree, bundle_dir, out)`: one tree's QA code on another build's geometry bundle (the 2x2's crossed cells): `python -m charkit qa` run in ...
- `twobytwo(base, cand, old_on_new, new_on_old, remeasured, accept=(), detected=())`: the 2x2 for each remeasured check: its value and status in base (the old geometry, the old measure), old_on_new ...
- `unmeasured_cells(r)`: a 2x2 row's crossed cells that couldn't be measured -> their names: the old measure on the new geometry when the ...
- `part_owners(meas, *reports)`: the checks each changed QA part owns -> {part: [checks] or None (unknown)}: the part's own record in a build's ...
- `measure_moved(base, cand, old_on_new, new_on_old, names)`: the checks (of names) that the two measures read differently on the same geometry, on the old or the new one -> ...
- `draw_exports(qa_a, qa_b)`: both QAs must draw from the same kind of export (the look export): a --vrm candidate that had none drew from its ...
- `geometry(out)`: a build's geometry, for the 2x2's "did the geometry change": its bundle's array hashes (charkit/bundle.py's ...
- `twobytwo_drops(rows)`: the 2x2's hidden regressions: a remeasured check that reads worse on the new geometry under either measure alike ...
- `gate(branch, into='HEAD', spec='charkit/spec/clawd.json', args=(), ...)`
- `calibration_step(rep, tree, head, qa_a, qa_b, clock=None)`: the merged tree's calibration records against what the merge adds and remeasures (rep['calibration']), the ...
- `carry(branch, into='pipeline-3d', spec='charkit/spec/clawd.json', args=(), ...)`: the newest gate of the branch's tip (any branch name) into an ancestor H0 of INTO, carried over to INTO with no ...
- `judge(rep, qa_a, qa_b)`: Michael's policy K on a gate's comparison -> (verdict, blocking, report).
- `verdict_pre_k(rep)`: the verdict the gate gave before policy K (FAIL on any check worse or gone and the 2x2's drops, WARN on 1.5x the ...
- `summary(rep, md=None)`: the machine-readable summary (REPORT.summary.json): the verdict under K, what blocks, the report by kind.
- `rejudge(path)`: an earlier gate report (its json) read under policy K: the check rows it holds, the flags from the two builds' ...
- `main(args)`

#### `charkit/geomstage.py`

Geometry truth (docs/GEOM_TRUTH.md): a build stage's geometry is computed once, venv-side, and saved as a product (arrays and a JSON record); Blender only instantiates meshes, materials and modifiers from it; the evaluator (charkit.bodyeval) makes the same product in memory, so the two agree by construction. The garments are the pilot.

- class `SeamError`: build() asked Blender for something outside the recorded seam.
- class `recording`: with recording() as rec: garments.build(...) runs with the seam recorded (rec.events, rec.arrays).
- `record(A, specs, hull=None, spec_all=None, line=None)`: garments.build on assembly A (numpy, no Blender) with its seam recorded.
- `product(stage, rec, meta=None, checks=None)`: a product in memory from a recording: -> dict(meta (JSON-able: schema, stage, events), arrays {name: array}).
- `save(P, path)`: a product to one .npz (compressed); written to a temporary name first, so a reader never sees half a file.
- `load(path)`: -> the product saved at path (the arrays loaded).
- `digest(P)`: a product's content hash (its events and arrays).
- `replay(P, C)`: Blender: the recorded calls made for real, in order, onto character C (charkit.character.build's: its 'arm' and ...
- `finalize(P)`: Michael's call J (M4): each recorded garment's Solidify and Subdivision Surface applied at rest, venv-side ...
- `pieces(P, coarse=False)`: the product as the evaluator reads it: per object made (in order) dict(name, V, polys, uv (per vertex) or ...
- `blender_bytes(rgba)`: an image's pixels as a Blender byte image keeps them after to_blender_image's float write (row 0 = top), as ...
- `assembly_key(spec)`: the key character.assemble(spec) is kept under: the sections it reads (ASM_KEYS), with the files ASM_FILES name ...
- `assemble(spec, keep=True, log=None)`: character.assemble(spec), kept on disk (charkit/out/geom/cache/asm_NAME_KEY.pkl, assembly_key) and in memory: the ...
- `garments_product(A, specs, hull=None, spec_all=None)`: the garments stage's product for assembly A: garments.build recorded (numpy), with the body it was built on kept ...
- `garments_step(spec, path, log=print)`: venv-side, the build's garments stage: the character assembled as the Blender side assembles it ...
- `garments_instantiate(S, path)`: Blender, the garments stage from its product: the objects replayed onto the scene's character, and the body the ...

#### `charkit/hair.py`

Anime hair (docs/CHARKIT.md §2, hair): a hair volume fitted to the character's head (ray-cast from the head's centre, then thickened, hung below the jaw), and a hairstyle of designed locks laid over it: each lock a spline through a few control points (root, the S-bend, the tip) with a width profile that ends in a clean point; the silhouette set by a hem ...

- class `MeshVolume`: The hair volume from a generated hair surface (charkit.target3d: the 3D target's hair part, in world; the visual ...
- `catmull(P, n)`: a uniform Catmull-Rom curve through control points P (k, d), sampled at n + 1 points.
- `sweep(V, pts, width, th=0.34, w_root=0.55, s_max=0.28, tip=0.45, ...)`: one lock: a lens-section tube along a Catmull-Rom path through control points (az, el, r) on the volume, its ...
- class `Builder`
- `hem(S, az, V=None)`: the silhouette's tip elevation at an azimuth (degrees from the front, either side); from a generated shape where ...
- `generate(H, centre, target, style=None, volume=None)`: the hairstyle's meshes by layer (numpy): {name: (verts, faces, uvs)}, and the volume (for the normals proxy).
- `cap(V, NA=64, NE=26)`: the cap: a smooth shell just inside the volume V, under everything so no scalp shows (open over the face).
- `proxy_mesh(V, NA=72, NE=40)`: the volume as a mesh (the clumps take their normals from it). -> (verts, faces).

- `build(A, arm, style=None, colors=None, volume=None)`: Blender objects for the hairstyle on an assembled character A (charkit.character.assemble), parented to the head bone.

#### `charkit/head.py`

The anime head shape (docs/CHARKIT.md §2): a knob-driven analytic surface (spline profiles for the face and the back of the skull, an elliptical dome cranium, superellipse sections) that charkit/anime_head.py wraps MakeHuman's head topology onto.

- class `Pchip`: monotone cubic interpolation (Fritsch-Carlson) of (xs, ys); clamped outside. Slopes precomputed.
- `pchip(xs, ys, x)`
- class `Head`
- `jaw_z(H, a)`: the head's lower boundary height at azimuth a: the chin at the front, the jaw corner at the sides, the nape behind.

charkit sweep optimize: a fit's knobs tuned by a batch optimizer instead of an agent hand-stepping sweeps (sweep, read the table, design the next sweep: face on sweep 8, lapels 7+, hair shells 11 on 2026-10-01). #### `charkit/procs.py`

Builds that know their own processes: every Blender a charkit command starts is recorded in its output folder (`.pid.json`: pid, command, start time) while it runs, so a build can be listed and stopped by its own record, never by a pattern that would match another worktree's builds.

- `slots()`
- `available_gb()`: memory the machine can hand out now (free + inactive + speculative pages; macOS vm_stat), or None elsewhere.
- `build_slot(label='build')`: a machine-wide build slot held for the block: a whole build, its produced references, venv steps, Blender and QA ...
- `thread_cap()`: the threads each of a build's pools gets (numba, BLAS, OpenMP, llvmpipe: THREAD_VARS): CHARKIT_THREADS (a number, ...
- `thread_env(n=None)`: the environment that caps a process's pools at n threads (default thread_cap()), OpenMP's waits passive.
- `cap_threads()`: this process and what it starts capped (thread_env), called before numpy, numba or a BLAS loads (they read these ...
- `acquire_slot(label='build', poll=2.0, mem=None)`: take a machine-wide build slot once enough memory is free, waiting while all slots are held or memory is short -> ...
- `write(out, pid, label, cmd)`: record a process in `out`/.pid.json. -> the record's path.
- `record(out, pid, label, cmd)`: `out`/.pid.json names `pid` while the block runs.
- `run(cmd, out, label='build', slot=True, **kw)`: run a command to completion in a build slot, its pid recorded in `out`/.pid.json (removed when it ends) -> ...
- `records(roots=None)`: the recorded processes under charkit/out of this worktree and its sibling worktrees -> [(pidfile, record, alive)].
- `slot_holders()`: who holds the build slots now -> [(slot, 'pid label root')].
- `set_slots(args)`: show or set the machine's slot count (CHARKIT_BUILD_SLOTS in the environment still wins).
- `ps(args=())`
- `wait(args)`: block until the build recorded in an output folder ends (its pid gone), or --timeout seconds pass; exit 0 when it ...
- `kill(args)`: stop the build recorded in an output folder (only that pid).

Review boards for charkit (docs/CHARKIT.md §4): orthographic and perspective views of a character or a lineup, rendered with a neutral clay or the character's own materials, composed into one image. from charkit import qa; qa.lineup([objs...], out_png, views=('front','side','three_q'))

#### `charkit/registry.py`

Self-registering QA parts and measurement steps (Michael, 2026-09-30: less coupling). There is no central list to edit, so two branches that each add a part or a step never conflict on one. How to register either is in docs/CHARKIT.md ("Registering a QA part or a measurement step").

- `qa_part(name, order, prefix='', table=None, ref_image=False, keep=None, ...)`: register the decorated function as the QA part `name` (see the module's docstring). -> the function.

- `is_flag(c)`: a check (its qa.json dict, or None) built from one of Michael's flags?

- `parts()`: every registered QA part, its module imported once -> [Part] by (order, name).
- `module_steps(path, name=STEPS_NAME)`: a file's module-level `NAME = [...]` literal, read with ast -> [(pattern, commit, why)], or None without one.
- `steps(root=HERE)`: every module's MEASUREMENT_STEPS under root (a charkit package directory, this tree's or another's), module by ...
- `step_sources(root=HERE)`: -> {module: its steps} (the registry's view by module, for tests and docs).

#### `charkit/sparse.py`

Sparse checkouts: what a worktree actually needs from the repo. The repo tracks about 1.8 GB, mostly other films' rig art and audio (projects/tsuzuku's 2D rigs alone are 1.4 GB), and every full worktree duplicates it; a charkit worktree reads a few hundred MB of it. #### `charkit/trace.py`

A build's state log, the way Dolphin's game-state log serves the Melee work: every stage writes what the scene holds after it, so a bad board traces back to the stage, object and number that made it, and two builds diff to exactly what changed.

- `faults(rec)`: an object record's health faults: {flag: count}, open edges left out for sheets.
- class `Trace`
- `begin(path, spec=None, spec_path=None, **env)`: open a trace at `path` (closing any open one) and write the build's environment.
- `resume(path)`: reopen a build's trace to append to it (the venv's QA after the Blender stage): its records continue the clock, ...
- `end()`
- `active()`
- `last()`: the last stage record written (None without a trace).
- `capture()`: collect the records written inside the block (a cached step replays them on a hit).
- `replay(recs, **extra)`: write captured records again (their own time stamps dropped), each with `extra`.
- `note(name, **values)`
- `event(kind, name=None, **values)`
- `span(name, **values)`: time a sub-step; values given up front, plus any the body adds to the yielded dict.
- `stage(name, S=None, objects=None)`: time a scene stage, then record what it did to the scene: objects added, changed (geometry hash moved) or ...
- `health(V, F, eps_area=1e-12)`: mesh health of (V (n, 3), F): see the module docstring. -> dict of counts.
- `geometry_hash(V, F=None, decimals=5)`: a short hash of the positions (rounded, so float noise below 10 um doesn't count) and the faces.
- `measuring(obs, skip=OUTLINE_MODS)`: the objects' `skip` modifiers off and one depsgraph update for all of them, so mesh_arrays inside needn't toggle ...
- `mesh_arrays(ob, evaluated=True, skip=OUTLINE_MODS, materials=False, uv=None)`: world-space (V, (loop verts, starts, counts)) of a mesh object; evaluated (modifiers, shape keys at their values) ...
- `object_snapshot(ob, prev=None)`: an object's record; health is only recomputed when its geometry hash moved since `prev`.
- `seed_health(records)`: reuse the health of object records already measured (a restored stage's), by geometry hash.
- `scene_snapshot(objects=None, prev=None, reuse=())`: every object's record; `reuse`: names whose record in `prev` stands (a restored stage left them as they were).
- `landmarks(S)`: the character's measured landmarks (world metres): head length, eye centres, mouth, chin, crown, the joints.
- `read(path)`
- `summary(recs)`: a text table per stage: time, objects, and per object added/changed its verts, faces and health.
- `diff(ra, rb, tol=0.0001, time_ratio=1.5)`: what changed from build A to build B, stage by stage: knob sections, landmarks, per-object geometry and health, ...
- `cache_summary(recs)`: one line: the stages and products restored and run, and the time the restores took.
- `main(args)`
- `portable(x, out=None)`: a spec value with the paths that name where it was built made portable, for hashing: a path in the build's own ...

#### `charkit/triage.py`

Residual triage: every check still WARN or FAIL after the tune loop, classified by why the loop couldn't fix it, with its evidence, and ranked into work items (docs/CHARKIT.md §4).

- `movers(check, tables, knobs, spec)`: what the fitters' sensitivity tables (charkit.fitkit's schema charkit.sensitivity/1, or a bare {knob: entry}) say ...
- `build_evidence(recs, check)`: what the tune's builds say about a check: rejected checkpoints that improved it while others regressed, and ...
- `uncertainty(check, c, ctx)`: -> (strong flags, soft flags): reasons to doubt the number.
- `reference(check, spec)`: the manifest's authority for the check's measure, and the reference it is measured against, with cautions.
- `classify(check, c, ctx)`: one residual check -> (class, detail sentence, evidence dict, also [other classes]).
- `tickets_path(spec)`
- `load_tickets(spec)`
- `items(qa, ctx, build_dir=None)`: the ranked work items for a build's QA -> list of dicts.
- `blocked_moves(recs, fitters)`: the rejected checkpoints that would have lowered the score: what each gained, and what blocked it, each blocking ...

- `run(end_ck, fits, fitters, cks, recs, cfg, spec, out_dir, ...)`: triage a tune run's end state -> {items, json, md, classes}; writes out_dir/work_items.json and .md and copies ...
- `from_dir(d, spec_path=None, config=None)`: re-triage a tune run's folder (from its tune.jsonl and the fits' tables) or a single build's.
- `main(args)`

#### `charkit/tune.py`

The tune loop: a character from its spec to a fitted, checked build, and whatever error is left turned into ranked work items (docs/CHARKIT.md §4).

- `say(*a)`
- `config_path(spec)`
- `load_config(spec, path=None)`
- `tradeoff(row, prev, cand, rules)`: the rule that allows this regressed row, with the check that paid for it -> (rule, gainer, gain, cost) or None.
- `accept(prev, cand, rules=(), remeasured=None, min_gain=0.001, authority=None)`: a candidate checkpoint's QA against the best so far -> {verdict accept|reject, why, gain, rows, regressed, ...
- `stop_reason(scores, all_pass=False, builds_left=None, seconds_left=None, ...)`: why the loop stops now, or None to go on.
- `all_pass(qa)`
- `failing(qa)`
- class `Record`: tune.jsonl: one JSON line per event.
- `read(path)`
- class `Checkpoint`: a built and QA'd state: {id, label, spec, args, out, boards, qa (loaded), score, summary, git, seconds, reused}.
- class `Builder`: full builds for the loop: `python -m charkit build` per checkpoint, with the history note; a checkpoint whose ...
- `worker_up(log=print)`: start the persistent Blender worker for this checkout when tool/speed has landed and none is running -> True when ...
- `worker_down()`
- `tune(spec_path, out=None, budget=None, review=False, args=(), only=None, ...)`
- `unallowed(regs, rules)`: predicted status regressions {check: [from, to]} that no trade-off rule's `allow` covers (within its floor).
- `agreement(rows, band=0.5)`: a fitter's validate() rows -> {check: [evaluator, build]} where their severities differ by more than `band`.
- `disagreement(fit, ck, start=None, band=0.5)`: checks where the fitter's fast evaluator measured a severity the full build's QA doesn't reproduce (by more than ...
- `main(args)`

#### `charkit/worker.py`

The build worker: one long-running Blender that keeps charkit loaded and takes build jobs over a local socket, so a build skips Blender's start-up and keeps its warm render state (shaders compiled, add-ons registered).

- `sock_path()`: the worker's socket: in its folder, or (a path too long for a Unix socket) in the temp folder, named by checkout.
- `info()`
- `request(msg, timeout=None)`: one request -> the worker's replies (None when no worker runs).
- `submit(job, out, label)`: run a build job (build_blender's arguments) in the worker, recorded in `out` under the worker's pid.
- `start(wait=60)`
- `stop(quiet=False, wait=20)`
- `status()`
- `rss_mb(pid)`: a process's resident memory, MB (None when it can't be read).
- `main(args)`

#### `charkit/worker_blender.py`

Blender entry for the build worker (charkit/worker.py): serve build jobs on a Unix socket until asked to stop. blender -b --factory-startup --python charkit/worker_blender.py -- SOCKET INFO.json

- `rss_mb()`
- `save_info()`
- `purge()`
- `clean()`: charkit unloaded, the scene at factory settings, Python's garbage collected.
- class `Stream`: a job's stdout and stderr, line by line to the client (and to the worker's log).
- `job(conn, msg, base)`
- `serve()`

### charkit/geom/

#### `charkit/geom/__init__.py`

charkit.geom: a deterministic geometry kernel for charkit (docs/GEOM.md), so the pipeline doesn't lean on Blender's modifiers for geometry. Runs in the venv (numpy, scipy, scikit-image, numba; manifold3d for exact booleans); the results (npz / ply / glb) go to the Blender stage, which needs only numpy to read them (charkit.geom.io, charkit.geom.blender).

#### `charkit/geom/blender.py`

The Blender side of charkit.geom: numpy + bpy only (no scipy / numba), so it runs in Blender's bundled Python.

- `load_part(path, name, material=None, normals='envelope', link=True)`: a saved part as a Blender mesh object (smooth shaded).
- `normals_proxy(path, name)`: a hidden copy of a saved part carrying its envelope normals as custom normals, for transfer_normals().
- `set_normals(ob, N, name='volume_normals')`: custom normals onto `ob`, exactly one per vertex: N (the mesh's vertices in order) kept as a point attribute and ...
- `transfer_normals(ob, proxy, name='volume_normals', mapping='NEAREST_NORMAL')`: custom normals onto `ob` from `proxy` (the same surface) by a Data Transfer modifier.

#### `charkit/geom/boolean.py`

Mesh booleans: exact and robust through manifold3d (Apache-2.0) when both inputs are manifold (closed, every edge on two faces, consistently wound), and through the volume path (voxel SDF booleans, marching cubes) otherwise: bodies with internal cavities, self-intersections, holes or double shells, where exact booleans refuse or fail.

- `to_manifold(m)`: a manifold3d.Manifold of a mesh (double precision), or None when manifold3d rejects it (not manifold).
- `from_manifold(M)`
- `is_manifold(m)`: closed, 2-manifold and consistently wound (what an exact boolean needs).
- `boolean(a, b, op='difference', h=None, fallback=True, exact=True)`: a op b.
- `volume_boolean(a, b, op='difference', h=None, band=3.0)`: the volume path: SDFs of both on one lattice (exact near the surfaces, sign by winding number), min / max, ...
- `union(a, b, **kw)`
- `difference(a, b, **kw)`
- `intersection(a, b, **kw)`

#### `charkit/geom/bvh.py`

A bounding-volume hierarchy over a triangle mesh and vectorised queries on large point / ray sets (numba, parallel over queries; deterministic: each query is independent).

- class `BVH`: a BVH over a mesh's triangles (leaf size `leaf`). The mesh's arrays are kept (not copied); don't mutate them.

#### `charkit/geom/det.py`

Deterministic numerics: the same bits on every machine, for code whose output feeds discrete decisions (a voxel in or out, which view a vertex takes, which edge collapses next).

- `snap(x, q)`: x to the nearest multiple of q (half to even); q a power of two, so x / q and the product back are exact.
- `cs(az)`: (cos, sin) of `az` degrees, the same on every machine.
- `kernel(sigma, truncate=4.0)`: scipy.ndimage's Gaussian kernel (radius int(truncate * sigma + 0.5), normalised), from libm's exp snapped and a ...
- `gaussian(x, sigma, mode='reflect', dtype=np.float32)`: a separable Gaussian blur of an N-d array (sigma a number or one per axis; 0 skips an axis), as ...
- `dot3(P, v)`: (N, 3) rows dotted with a 3-vector, in the order x, y, z -> (N,).
- `norm3(P)`: (N, 3) rows' lengths, in the order x, y, z -> (N,).
- `normals_area(V, F)`: unit vertex normals, the sum of the incident faces' (area-weighted) normals: cross products and sums in a fixed ...
- `normals_angle(V, F)`: unit vertex normals weighted by each face's corner angle (mesh.vertex_normals' default, the pseudo-normal): the ...
- `nearest(P, Q, k=8)`: the index into P of each Q's nearest point, the same on every machine.

#### `charkit/geom/headmesh.py`

A head's topology authored in code: a quad cage with concentric loops round each eye and round the mouth, and a neck of rings under the jaw, so the head is all quads with the edge flow a face rig needs (lid loops, a mouth loop) and nothing inherited from a scanned or realistic base. charkit.geom.headfit gives it the design's shape.

- `lines(lo, hi, cell, must=())`: monotone grid lines from lo to hi about `cell` apart, passing through every value of `must` inside.
- class `Cage`
- `cage(X, Y, Z, features=(), neck=None)`: the head's cage on the box lattice of grid lines X, Y, Z (L).
- `edges(F)`: directed edges of quads -> (E, 2).
- `check(C)`: the cage's health: quads only, every edge in at most two faces and wound oppositely there, the boundary loops, ...
- `cylinder(nth, zs, dome, place, dome_place, features=(), cap=True)`: the head's cage on a cylinder chart: columns at nth angles round the head (theta_j = 2 pi j / nth - pi, 0 = the ...
- `compact(C)`: a cage without the vertices no face uses (a feature block's inner lattice points), its loops remapped.

#### `charkit/geom/io.py`

#### `charkit/geom/mesh.py`

The triangle mesh the kernel passes around, and its topology (numpy only).

- class `Mesh`
- `as_mesh(m, F=None)`
- `face_normals(V, F, unit=True)`: (M,3) face normals (unit, or area-weighted: |n| = 2 * area when unit=False).
- `face_areas(V, F)`
- `vertex_normals(V, F, weight='angle')`: per-vertex unit normals: 'angle' (angle-weighted: the pseudo-normal, good for signs) or 'area'.
- `corner_angles(V, F)`: (M,3) interior angles of each triangle at its corners.
- `signed_volume(V, F)`: the enclosed volume (positive for a closed, outward-oriented mesh).
- `area(V, F)`
- `edge_lengths(V, F)`: (M,3) lengths of edges (F[:,k], F[:,k+1]).
- `half_edges(F)`: (3M,2) directed edges (a -> b) in face order: row 3*f + k is (F[f,k], F[f,k+1]).
- `unique_edges(F)`: -> (E (K,2) sorted undirected edges, inverse (3M,) half-edge -> edge index, count (K,) faces per edge).
- `edge_key(a, b, n)`: a unique int64 key per undirected edge (for n vertices).
- `boundary_edges(F)`: directed boundary half-edges (used by exactly one face), oriented as in their face.
- `nonmanifold_edges(F)`
- `face_adjacency(F, manifold_only=True)`: pairs of faces sharing an edge: (P (K,2) face pairs, edge (K,2) the shared vertex pair).
- `components(F, nv=None, by='vertex')`: connected components: -> (labels, count).
- `vertex_adjacency(F, n)`: the symmetric vertex adjacency as a scipy CSR matrix (1 per edge).
- `boundary_loops(F)`: the boundary as closed loops of vertex indices (lists), following the faces' orientation.
- `compact(m, keep_faces=None)`: drop faces (keep_faces: bool (M,) or indices) and then unused vertices; per-vertex attributes follow.
- `concatenate(meshes)`: one mesh from several (attributes kept only when every mesh has them).
- `transform(m, M)`: apply a 4x4 (or 3x3) matrix to the vertices (normals rotated by the inverse transpose).
- `y_up_to_z_up(m)`: glTF's frame (y up, -z forward...
- `z_up_to_y_up(m)`

#### `charkit/geom/raster.py`

A small z-buffer rasteriser in numpy/numba for measuring and looking at meshes without Blender: orthographic views round the character by azimuth (qa3d's convention: az 0 looks at the front from -y, x to the right, z up), silhouettes, IoU, and shaded renders (flat / lambert / toon from per-vertex normals, optional vertex colours) saved as PNG.

- class `Frame`: an orthographic framing: world centre (x, z at the image centre), `scale` world units across the image height, ...
- `rasterize(m, az, frame, el=0.0)`: -> (zbuffer (H,W) inf where empty, face id (-1), barycentrics (H,W,3)).
- `silhouette(m, az, frame, el=0.0)`
- `window_shape(pix, win)`: a measuring window's pixel size (W, H): win = dict(x, top, bottom) in units of L round the origin.
- `window_project(V, az, origin, L, pix, win)`: world points -> (pixel x, y (N, 2), depth along the view (N,), smaller = nearer) on a measuring window: the ...
- `facing(V, T, az)`: per triangle: does its front (counter-clockwise winding, Blender's) face a camera at azimuth az?
- `window_zbuffer(meshes, az, origin, L, pix, win, ids=False, thin=())`: the nearest surface per pixel centre of a measuring window (window_project), every mesh occluding every other: ...
- `iou(a, b)`
- `overlay(a, b)`: an RGB float image: both (grey), only a (red), only b (blue).
- `render(items, az, frame, el=0.0, bg=(0.93, 0.93, 0.95), light=(-0.45, 0.55, ...)`: shade meshes together (one z-buffer).
- `save_png(img, path)`
- `sheet(images, cols=None, pad=4, bg=1.0)`: tile equally sized images (H, W, 3) into one.

#### `charkit/geom/remesh.py`

Remeshing and decimation on a small dynamic triangle mesh in numba (sequential, deterministic):

- `isotropic(m, target, iters=5, project=True, relax=0.8, cos_min=0.3, verbose=False)`: isotropic remeshing to edge length `target` (Botsch and Kobbelt 2004).
- `decimate(m, target_faces, cos_min=0.2, max_edge=None, max_cost=None, info=None)`: quadric-error edge collapse down to `target_faces` (or as far as the checks allow).

#### `charkit/geom/subsurf.py`

Blender's Subdivision Surface, in numpy (Michael's call J: subdivision leaves Blender). - `refine(V, T, es, vs, rotate=True)`: one level of Catmark.

- `level_dependent(T, es, vs)`: the vertices whose limit mask gives a different point at a finer level: darts (a smooth vertex on one infinitely ...
- `evaluate(V, T, es, vs, level, quality=3, rings=4)`: the positions Blender's modifier gives T's vertices (T at `level` of refinement): the limit masks at the adaptive ...

#### `charkit/render/__init__.py`

charkit's own toon renderer (docs/workstreams/toonrender.md): the look's cel shading drawn from a build's export on the GPU through wgpu, headless, with no Blender in the loop. It draws the boards as EEVEE does (the same cameras, light, screen-width lines, face shadow, streaks, features through the hair, film filter), about a hundred times faster.

Venv-side posing for the motion pilot: the skeleton's skinning matrices from a build's joints (bundle landmarks) and motion QA's poses (charkit.evalmesh.POSES: per bone a rotation (axis, degrees) in the armature's frame about its head, composed down the chain, as evalmesh.motion_main poses Blender's armature), linear blend skinning, and capsule colliders ...

- `rest_clear(caps, P, margin)`: the capsules shrunk so none holds a point of P at rest (the cloth's own rest vertices: a collider that already ...
- `transfer_weights(P, V, F, W)`: skin weights carried to points P from the nearest point of the mesh (V, F) with vertex weights W {bone: (n,)} ...
- `capsule_rows(caps, D, shrink=0.0)`: the capsules posed by D: rows (a, b, r, r) for xpbd.Capsules (radius less `shrink`).

#### `charkit/sim/settings.py`

The cloth solver's settings from a style profile (charkit/styles: the `physics` section, declared ahead of the solvers). Physics is the baseline; every departure is a named, deterministic dial (docs/CHARKIT_HANDOFF.md, "Production principles": liberties are dials, tuned in code).

- `physics(style='anime', **over)`: the profile's physics section with the solver's dials: -> dict.

#### `charkit/sim/springbone.py`

VRMC_springBone chains, simulated as the VRM 1.0 spec describes (a verlet tail per joint: inertia less drag, a pull toward the rest direction carried by the parent (stiffness), gravity, the bone's length kept, sphere and capsule colliders pushing the tail out by its hit radius; each bone then aimed at its tail). The rig's spring chains for the flaps and ...

- `from_to(a, b)`: the smallest rotation taking direction a onto direction b.
- class `Chain`
- `arc_param(P, J)`: each point's place along the polyline J (0 ..

#### `charkit/sim/waist.py`

The waistband's weights at motion QA's poses (round 3, decision 1: the waistband takes the body's weights near the waist). Variants: rigid on the hips (a belt's default), the body's per vertex (garments.body_weights: the spec's `"weights": "body"`), per column (each column's rows averaged: no shear across the band), one blend for the whole band. Per ...

- `run(build, out, pictures=('squat', 'twist_bend'), log=print)`

#### `charkit/sim/xpbd.py`

XPBD cloth (Macklin, Mueller and Chentanez 2016, "XPBD: position-based simulation of compliant constrained dynamics"; Macklin et al. 2019, "Small steps in physics simulation": many substeps, one Gauss-Seidel pass each). numpy for the set up, numba for the constraint loops; single-threaded and in a fixed order, so a run is bit-identical to any re-run.

- `triangulate(polys)`: polygons (lists of vertex indices) -> triangles (fan from each polygon's first corner).
- `edges_and_hinges(F)`: the unique edges of triangles F, and per interior (two-triangle) edge its hinge (a, b, c, d): a-b the shared ...
- `dihedral(x0, x1, x2, x3)`
- class `Cloth`: a piece of cloth: rest positions V (m), triangles, edges and hinges, lumped masses (area density `density`, ...
- class `Capsules`: tapered capsules: rows (a (3), b (3), ra, rb), set per frame (the rig carries them).
- class `SDFGrid`: a signed-distance grid (negative inside) over a box, carried rigidly (R, t: world = R local + t).
- class `Solver`: a cloth under gravity with kinematic pins and hold targets, colliders and layers.
- `rotation(axis, ang)`: Rodrigues: the rotation by `ang` radians about `axis`.

Board: a character spec (charkit/spec/*.json) built and rendered in charkit's look: head views (front, 30, 60, 90, 150), an expression row and a mouth row; next to the spec's reference art when it has one. blender -b --factory-startup --python charkit/boards/char_board.py -- SPEC.json OUTDIR [--fast]

Board: the face in charkit's toon look: head close-ups (85 mm: front, 30, 60, 90 degrees) and an expression row (the lid shapes at the front), with the eyes, lashes and the skin's outline. blender -b --factory-startup --python charkit/boards/face_board.py -- OUTDIR [variant ...]

### charkit/styles/

#### `charkit/styles/__init__.py`

Style profiles: the settings that make charkit build a character in one 3D style rather than another (Michael, 2026-09-28: the kit is meant for any 3D style, not only anime). Every stage that assumes something about a style reads it from the character's profile (spec['style'], default 'anime') instead of hard-coding it.

- `names()`
- `merge(base, over)`: `over` laid over `base` (a copy), dict by dict: a nested section keeps what `over` doesn't set.
- `load(name='anime')`: a style profile: DEFAULT with the named profile's sections laid over it.
